                   ﻭﺩ ﺮﻫ ﺖﻓﺍ ﻦﯾﺮﺘﺸﯿﺑ ﻝﺎﺣ ﻦﯾﺍ ﺎﺑ .(۰٫۳۳۶۵ ﺮﺑﺍﺮﺑ ﺭﺩ ۰٫۴۵۳۳) ﺪﻫﺩ ͬﻣ ﻥﺎﺸﻧ ﯼﺮﺗ ﮒﺭﺰﺑ ﺖﻗﺩ ﯼﺮﺗﺮﺑ

                   ﯼﺎﻫەﺯﺎﺑ .ﺖﺴﯿﻧ ͬﻧﺎﺷﻮﭘ ﻢﻫ ﯼەﻠﺌﺴﻣ ﻂﻘﻓ ﺮͽﯾﺩ ﻪﻠﺌﺴﻣ ﻭ ﺖﺴﯿﻧ ͬﻓﺎﮐ ﻒﯾﺎﻇﻭ ﯼەﻤﻫ ﯼﺍﺮﺑ ﻪͺﺒﺷ ﺖﯿﻓﺮﻇ

                         .ﺩﺮﯾﺬﭘ ͬﻣ ﺍﺭ ﺎﻫ ﻥﺁ ﺖﯿﻟﻮﺌﺴﻣ ﻭ ﻩﺩﺮﮐ ͬﻨﯿﺑﺯﺎﺑ ﺍﺭ ﺞﯾﺎﺘﻧ
                        ۹۱
                                                  Abstract
                 Continual learning systems deployed in real-world environments must often honor
              requests to unlearn specific information. In task-incremental settings, selective for-
              getting requires suppressing a designated target task while maintaining performance
              on all retained tasks. The central obstacle is parameter overlap: when tasks share
              underlying model parameters, resetting or repairing weights for one task frequently
              degrades the others. The Privacy-Aware Lifelong Learning (PALL) framework mod-
              ularizes task knowledge through stored subnetworks, but its request-time reset and
              repair do not distinguish shared coordinates by their sensitivity to the retained tasks.
                 This thesis introduces EPALL, an enhanced PALL procedure that identifies the
              shared support between the target subnetwork and the union of the active subnet-
              works, ranks those coordinates by retained-task gradient magnitude, and anchors
              the high-sensitivity subset with an ℓ2 penalty during the repair phase. In addition,
              an exploratory parameter-eﬀicient pathway, PALL-Adapter, is designed and audited
              through matched component controls.
                 OnSplit-CIFARbenchmarks,aneleven-methodeight-seedcomparisonshowsthat
              EPALL drives forgotten-task accuracy to the chance level (0.4863 on CIFAR-10 and
              0.0959 on CIFAR-100) while matching the full-isolation CLPU reference in retained
              accuracy to within seed spread. Against the original PALL algorithm, the paired
              accuracy difference is positive and excludes zero on both datasets (exact one-sided
              Wilcoxon p = 0.0078, the attainable floor at eight seeds), and WorstDrop on CIFAR-
              100 falls from 0.0222 to 0.0056.  A controlled 120-run benchmark over five pre-
              specified overlap levels shows that PALL-Original’s WorstDrop grows with overlap
              while EPALL stays comparatively flat, and resident-state accounting shows CLPU
              using 1.6–2.2× as much accounted state.
                 Matched component controls narrow the mechanism claim, and these limits are
              reported explicitly: what the evidence supports is retention-sensitivity ranking under
              a fixed budget, whereas the explicit mask-intersection filter is redundant and the
              anchor term shows no measurable independent contribution. For PALL-Adapter,
              the controls attribute task suppression primarily to target-path reset rather than to
              the overlap-aware soft mask. No result establishes exact, certified, or storage-level
              removal, and all claims are empirical.
              Keywords:
                 Continual Learning, Machine Unlearning, Selective Forgetting, Parameter Over-
              lap, Protected Subnetworks, Parameter-Eﬀicient Fine-Tuning
                       Sharif University of Technology
                     Department of Computer Engineering
                           M.Sc. Thesis
         Overlap-Aware Selective Forgetting in Continual
